Largest Company on Earth — Odds vs. Reality

Polymarket runs books on which company finishes as the world's largest by market cap. This tab now tracks August 31, September 30 and December 31, 2026 — the July block was resolved (NVIDIA) and has been retired from the tab in favour of the live September ladder. This page compares live Polymarket odds against a probability model driven by the actual market-cap gap, flags where the odds don't reflect reality, and measures how fast the odds move. Aug 1, 2026 — model correction: the fair column now prices the event these books actually resolve on, P(this company is the largest), computed by quadrature over the same diffusion. It used to price P(this company passes today's leader) and back out the leader as a residual — a union bound that double-counted overtakes and understated the leader by 6 pts in August and 24.5 pts in December. Live plumbing on this page

Idea added Jul 26, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

Odds: Polymarket CLOB GET /midpoint?token_id=… per contract (no auth, CORS-open) every 60s. Caps: Finnhub quotes × fixed share counts, refreshed on the same tick. Model fair values, edges and verdict badges recompute client-side on every refresh. As of Aug 1 the fair column is exact-rank: each name carries idiosyncratic log-vol σ/√2 (so any pair’s log-ratio keeps the published σ = 2%/day, and any common market factor cancels in a ranking), and P(X is the largest) is integrated over X’s own shock by 481-node Simpson quadrature on [−8, 8] — about 1 ms per book. It replaces the pairwise construction (challenger = P(chal > leader), leader = 100 − Σ), which was a union bound and understated the leader by 6.3 pts in August and 24.5 in December. Trading-day countdown with the US holiday calendar is unchanged. Once the July markets settle on-chain their midpoint endpoints go dark — the July rows above then hold tonight’s baked terminal prints permanently. If either API is unreachable the page falls back to the baked snapshot and the pill in the header flips from LIVE to SNAPSHOT.

Methodology & Honest Caveats

Model change, Aug 1 2026 — read this first. Until tonight the fair column answered the wrong question. It priced each challenger as P(chal ends above today’s leader) = Φ(ln(capchal/caplead) / σ√T) and then defined the leader’s own leg as 100 − Σ(challengers) − small legs. These books do not resolve on “did you pass the leader”; they resolve on “are you the largest”. The two differ because the passing events overlap — one bad quarter for the leader pushes several challengers past at once — so Σ(challengers) is a union bound that overstates P(someone passes), and the entire error landed in the residual. Measured bias: the leader leg read 6.3 pts low in August and 24.5 pts low in December, and the challengers carried an equal and opposite error (December: Apple +9.2, Alphabet +10.9, Microsoft +3.8, Amazon +0.6 — summing to exactly the leader’s 24.5), which is where the page’s long-running “+31.7 on NVIDIA-December” came from. The replacement keeps the identical diffusion and the identical published σ, and just does the integral. Each name gets idiosyncratic log-vol σ/√2, so every pair’s log-ratio still carries σrel = 2%/day and any common market factor cancels (it must — a ranking is scale-invariant). Then P(X largest) = ∫ φ(z) Πj≠X Φ([ln(capX/capj) + s·z]/s) dz with s = (σ/√2)√T, evaluated by 481-node Simpson quadrature on [−8, 8] in the browser on every refresh (≈1 ms). It agrees with a 2 000 000-path Monte Carlo of the same process to 0.05 pt, and its legs sum to 100 by construction rather than by subtraction. Second- and third-place fairs come from the same simulation and are re-baked each run; they are no longer the independence-assuming products this box used to warn about. T is in trading days (7 to Aug 31 / 28 to Sep 30 / 92 to Dec 31, counted from the Aug 21 UTC snapshot date; holidays Sep 7, Nov 26, Dec 25). The gap-vol rule adopted after the AMZN miss stands: any single-name leg with an earnings date inside its window gets a σ×2 sensitivity case (NVDA reports ≈ Aug 26 — now just two sessions inside the August book; Micron reports Sep 29, outside it — the August MU ladder is priced on realised vol, not event vol). What is still wrong with this model. It assumes no persistent relative drift — precisely the assumption the market rejects — so treat “edge” as model-vs-market disagreement, not profit. It assumes zero correlation in the idiosyncratic shocks, which is right for ranks but wrong if the names de-correlate asymmetrically. A σ = 3%/day sensitivity case moves August’s NVIDIA fair from 99.18% to 95.75% and December’s from 70.82% to ≈57.3% — that band is wider than most of the edges now on the page, and it is the honest reason nothing here is sized above $350. The parameter this model does not pin down — read before sizing anything. The published σ = 2%/day was only ever calibrated on the four leader-vs-challenger pairs, because that is all the old formula used. The exact-rank integral needs all ten pairs, and the six challenger-vs-challenger vols are free. Holding the leader pairs at exactly 2.0%/day and sweeping the challenger-challenger vol across its admissible range moves the answer more than the σ sensitivity does: on the Aug 18 caps December NVIDIA ran roughly 61.0–86.4 across 1.3–2.8%/day — a ~25-point band that brackets the 72.5 market. The sweep has NOT been re-run on the Aug 20 caps — and this walk sharpened the point in a new way: with the caps frozen and the tape shut, the clock alone moved every fair on the page (the December leader 69.7 → 70.82, the September 2nd seat 71.8 → 72.67, sep2NVDA 10.05 → 9.66 — enough to retire a card outright). A parameter band wider than the edges is one problem; a fair that drifts a point per calendar day is another, and both argue the same way about sizing. The sweep’s value as a filter was demonstrated overnight: NVDA-3rd-September’s NO-fair spans 96.1–99.2 across it, and the NO — which traded 87–88, under the band’s floor — converged to 95 in one overnight session, unexecuted (retired row 67); the Alphabet-Sept-crown NO inherits the sweep-proof slot (fair band 95.8–100.0 vs a 97.9¢ NO cost tonight). The MSFT-3rd-Sept leg the account bought Aug 6 is the opposite case: its fair sweeps 1.3–15.1 around a 7.3¢ fill (mark 7.95 this morning, dead on the un-swept 7.7 fair), the widest relative band on the page, which is why it stays a punt and why its epilogue card retired on the Aug 20 evening walk (row 102). Apple, Alphabet, Microsoft and Amazon co-move with each other more than any of them co-moves with NVIDIA, which argues the true challenger-challenger number is below 2%/day — i.e. the model as published is, if anything, too generous to the second-place trades and too harsh on the December leader. This is the single largest known uncertainty on the page and it is not in any of the numbers above. Small legs are now read off the books, not guessed. The untracked-competitor allowance was 1.25 (Aug) and 3.65 (Dec) with no stated source; the actual quotes are Tesla 0.15 + Aramco 0.25 + Broadcom 0.05 in August (0.45) and Tesla 0.15 + SpaceX 0.80 + Aramco 0.25 in December (1.20 — all re-read this walk off their own books, all unchanged; Tesla-December prints 0.15 and SpaceX-December 0.9). Correcting it moved every leader leg ≈0.6 pt and pushed two tickets under the actionability bar — a reminder that a parameter nobody checks is a parameter that is wrong. September is baked, not live. The 60-second loop now covers August, September and December; every September figure on this page is re-simulated by hand each run (T = 28 trading days this run, 2,000,000-path Monte Carlo for the rank-2/rank-3 fairs) and does not update between runs. Empirical odds-move stats use the rolling ~31-close prices-history window (21 per-run prints for the September book). Mid prices are not executable — every ticket is sized against a walked book. Odds and books are the Aug 21, 2026 13:35 UTC Friday MORNING walk; caps are the Aug 20 Finnhub closing quotes, unchanged from the previous walk because the equity market was shut end to end between them. July resolved NVIDIA and its block has been retired from this tab; on-chain settlement is still unobserved at this snapshot — the July books continue to return quotes. Nothing here is financial advice; prediction markets can resolve against you on oracle judgment alone.

Treasuries — The 6% Long Bond

The 30-year Treasury closed 5.23% on Aug 20, 2026 — a 4 bp give-back of the Aug 19 buyback rally, 8 bp under the Aug 17 record — leaving 77 bp to a 6-handle that has not printed since May 2000. This page hunts every Polymarket book that pays off if the 30-year touches 6.00% before Dec 31, 2026, prices each one against a disclosed model, and maps what a 6% long bond does to the economy and the stock market. There is no direct 30-year market on Polymarket — the closest tradeable instrument is the 10-year “how high” ladder, plus the Fed-hike complex, the SPX down-touch ladder, and the macro side bets. Odds refresh live from the Polymarket CLOB every 60 seconds; books re-walked Aug 21, 2026 19:08 UTC (Friday afternoon). Three things changed tonight, and two of them are corrections to this page. First, the yield pipeline is fixed: federalreserve.gov H.15 and Treasury.gov par yields now feed the run directly, so the curve is current for the first time since Jul 30 — and it shows this page has been quoting a stale record (the 2026 high as of Aug 10 was 5.27% on Jul 31 — since taken out by 5.31 on Aug 17 — not the 5.21% led with here for eleven days). Second, P(30Y touches 6%) is cut from 18% to 5.4%: re-derived on the new curve, two-thirds of the old number was drift extrapolated from a coefficient with a t-statistic of 0.84, and the old 18% never reproduced from its own stated inputs. Third, and most important, the “largest edge on leadsure.com” is withdrawn — an equal-weight mixture of the two models that actually fit this series prices the 5.0% rung at 35.0 against a 34.00 market and the 5.2% rung at 13.8 against 14.15. The ladder did double on the day (the 5.0% anchor went 17.0 → 34.0 and the position marks +$1,015), but the bid side of that book is 1,047 shares and $178.67 against an 8,880-share position: 88% of it has no bid at any price. Fictitious research page — not financial advice.

#2 · THE EDGE IS GONE RETIRED — WAS #1 SINCE JUL 31 A 50/50 mixture of the two defensible models prices rung 1 at 35.0 vs a 34.00 market and rung 2 at 13.8 vs 14.15 — the market is the average, not the mistake

Idea added Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

This tab has carried "the largest edge on leadsure.com" for ten days on the strength of a −24.7 gap between a 41.7% scenario fair and a 17¢ book. Tonight it collapses, and not because the book moved. Two models survive scrutiny on this series: a zero-drift random walk (49.1 at the 5.0% strike) and a fitted AR(1) (20.9). Their equal-weight mixture gives 35.0 against a 34.00 market, and at the 5.2% strike 13.8 against 14.15. Both live rungs are priced inside a point. An efficient market facing genuine model uncertainty should look exactly like the average of the candidate models — and this one does.

THE TICKET: The CHEAP call on rungs 1 and 2 is withdrawn. Not downgraded — withdrawn. The account holds 11,748 shares across the two of them at a blended basis inside both fairs, so the position stands; the claim does not. Nothing on this tab is actionable at the mid.

#5 · THE LADDER PRICES A SMILE THE OLD RESULT WAS AN ARTEFACT Implied vol runs 1.94 bp/day at the 4.8% strike to 5.87 at 6.00% — a constant-σ model mechanically calls near rungs cheap and far rungs rich

Idea added Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

Invert each rung to the volatility that makes it fair at zero drift and the book stops looking irrational: 4.8% → 1.94, 5.0% → 3.11, 5.2% → 3.38, 5.5% → 4.48, 5.7% → 4.97, 6.00% → 5.87 bp/day. That is a clean, monotone smile — the market pays up for tail vol and marks down near-barrier diffusion, which is what every options market on earth does. This tab has been fitting a single constant σ across all six strikes. Against a rising smile a constant σ must call the near rungs cheap and the far rungs rich. That is precisely the pattern this tab has reported for ten days and read as a finding. It was an assumption looking at itself.

THE TICKET: No trade. The verdict column on the ladder rows below is now computed against the mixture, and the implied-σ column is published so the smile is visible rather than laundered through a fair. A constant-σ fair is quoted only for the 5.5%+ rungs, where the account holds no view worth defending.

The tab learned tonight that inverting each rung to its own implied volatility is the honest way to read a ladder. Run it on all three ladders on the page, same 143-day clock, same driftless touch formula, barrier distances as already published here. The 10-year: 1.94, 3.11, 3.38, 4.48, 4.97, 5.87 bp/day — monotone up. The SPX down-touches: 24.6% at −17%, 27.9% at −23%, 33.3% at −31% — monotone up, a textbook equity put skew. Gold: 42.1% at +22% and 36.2% at +46% — down. Two markets pay up for the tail and one marks it down, and the one that marks it down is the market whose only reason to exist is a monetary accident. Caveat stated plainly: gold monitors on CME settlements and SPX on 1-minute candles, so the cross-ladder levels are not comparable. The slope inside gold is, and it is the wrong sign.

2 · THE KILL-SWITCH GOVERNS A $178.67 BOOK FROM A $153,211 ONE Sept-hike 43.5 on 372,401.1 shares of bids inside 40¢ — 857× the ladder it controls

Noted Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

Put the two books side by side and the tab’s whole posture looks strange. The September hike leg quotes 43 × 18,254 / 44 × 74,993, and inside 40¢ its bid stack holds 372,401.1 shares and $153,211.0. The 5.0% rung it governs has a bid side of 1,046.7 shares and $178.67. The ratio is 857×. The page has spent eleven days extracting signal from the thin book and using the deep one only as an on/off switch — and the switch is still FIRED at 1.5 points under its 45¢ trigger, re-arm requiring two full closes above it. The observation is not that the switch is wrong. It is that a market with $153,211 of visible bids is the one that should be traded and a market with $178.67 is the one that should be inferred from — and the tab has had it backwards.

3 · THE HIKE COMPLEX IS COHERENT TO WITHIN THE SPREAD 43.5 + 32.5 against 58.5 forces P(hikes at both meetings) ≥ 17.5 — a ≥40.2% conditional, and history says that is conservative

Noted Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

Three walked books, one arithmetic constraint. Sept-hike prints 43.5, the Dec 8–9 leg 32.5, any-hike-in-2026 58.5. Since a hike at either meeting implies a hike in the year, P(A∪B) ≤ 58.5, so the books require P(hikes at both) ≥ 17.5 points — a conditional of at least 40.2% given a September move, and that is before the Oct 27–28 meeting is counted at all, which only pushes the implied joint higher. Is 40.2% plausible? In every modern tightening cycle a first hike was followed by another inside two meetings far more often than not. So the complex is not just coherent, it is coherent with room to spare — no arbitrage, no dislocation, nothing to fade. That is the finding: a deep book that survives its own inequality is evidence the noise on this tab is coming from somewhere else, and card 6 names where.

4 · THE BOOKS SAY 80.0% OF RECESSIONS COME WITH AN INTERMEETING CUT No-cuts 85.7 → all-cut probability 14.3, of which the unscheduled leg is 6.0 · recession prints 7.5

Noted Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

Four walked books, read as one distribution. No-cuts at 85.7 (85.6 × 261.5 / 85.8 × 4,209.7) puts all 2026 cut probability at 14.3. The emergency leg at 6.0 (5 × 1,288.1 / 7 × 313.6) is 42.0% of that — the market says nearly half of all easing, if it comes, arrives outside a scheduled meeting. Against a recession leg at 7.5 that implies 80.0% of recession-worlds carry an unscheduled cut. History is thinner than that: unscheduled easing clusters in 1998, 2001, 2007–08 and 2020, and several genuine recessions produced none at all. The tab’s own transmission map already wrote the modern answer — the BoE bought gilts with Bank Rate untouched, SVB got the BTFP. Facilities, not cuts. This is the cleanest priced disagreement on the tab, and it is why the emergency leg is the #3 ticket rather than a footnote.

5 · THREE UNRELATED TAILS, ONE PRICE Recession 7.5 · SPX touches −31% 7.5 · the 10-year touches 5.5% 7.35 — three different worlds quoted to within 0.2 of a point

Noted Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

An NBER-or-two-negative-quarters recession, a 1-minute candle 31% below the index, and a 78 bp back-up in the 10-year all print between 7.35 and 7.5 tonight. They are not the same event and they do not have the same shape: the recession leg is a slow data event resolved by BEA advance estimates, the SPX leg is an instantaneous wick, and the rate leg is a first-passage on a series with 4.3 bp/day of volatility. Their books could not be more different either — recession offers 6,190.2 shares of bids at 7¢, the SPX leg 12,219.3, the 10-year rung 181.3 shares inside 3.4¢. When independent markets converge on a single number it usually means one of them is being priced off the others by traders who have not read the resolution text. Two of the three carry real depth, which is the only reason this row is an observation and not a spread trade.

6 · THE MID IS OURS AND THE TAPE DISAGREES BY 7 POINTS Rung 1 mid 34.0, last trade 27.0, our own 698.02-share lift at 25.21¢ thirty-five minutes before the snapshot

Noted Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

Restated because it now governs ten tickets rather than one card. At 21:54Z the account cleared the rung-1 offer stack for 698.02 shares at a 25.21¢ VWAP; at 22:29Z the book read 31 × 43 / 37 × 100 with the next ask at 55. The 34.0 midpoint is the arithmetic mean of a bid nobody has hit and an offer we created by removing everything under it. The tape says 27.0. The gap between those two numbers is the single most useful fact on this tab, because it is the same gap that makes the resting offers in tickets #1 and #4 sensible: an 18-point air pocket above the touch is a standing invitation to whoever sells into the next buyer who behaves the way we did tonight. The page manufactured the dislocation; the page can also be on the other side of it.

7 · MARKED $3,582.74, REALISABLE $268.35 — A 92.5% HAIRCUT And the ratio is worst exactly where the position is biggest

Noted Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

Leg by leg, mark against what the bid stacks actually pay: rung 1 1,046.7 sh / $178.67 against 8,880.3 held, rung 2 a real stack of 173.0 sh / $16.85 inside 9.7¢ before a cliff to 3.5¢ against 2,868.2 held, rung 3 181.3 sh / $6.63 inside 3.4¢ against 1,812.5 held. Total mark $3,582.74, total realisable $268.35, haircut 92.5%. What the tab did not say last run is the corollary: the mark is not wrong. These legs pay $1.00 a share at resolution, so $3,582.74 is a defensible expectation and $268.35 is a liquidity fact — the position is a hold-to-Dec-31 bet whose only early exits are the resting offers in #1 and #4. Publishing both numbers side by side, forever, is the correction. Calling either one “value” is the error.

Friday afternoon walk (19:08Z): Four rungs now walk, since rung 0 has a book: mark $2,738.55, realisable $615.82, haircut 77.5%. Leg by leg: rung 0 $211.94 → $194.71 (61.10¢), rung 1 $2,112.82 → $296.26 (3.52¢, 975 sh bidless), rung 2 $296.53 → $113.91, rung 3 $117.26 → $10.94. Rungs 1–3 alone: $2,526.61 / $421.11, 83.3%.

8 · THE INFLATION BARRIER IS A TENTH HIGHER THAN THE TICKER SAYS “Increased by greater than” plus one-decimal BLS reporting makes 4.5% resolve NO — and this is the only book on the tab with real depth on both sides

Noted Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

Two things nobody on this tab had checked. First, the resolution text: YES requires CPI to have “increased by greater than the listed percent”, resolved off a release that “reports inflation over 12 month periods to only one decimal point” — so a 4.5% print resolves NO and the effective barrier is 4.6%, a full tenth above the strike everyone quotes. Second, the book: 20 × 1,712.21 / 21 × 764.24, with $959.94 of bids inside 19¢ and $652.59 of offers inside 22¢. That is the only genuinely two-sided market on this entire tab — every other row is deep on one side, thin on the other, or a canyon. Headline is 3.5%, core 2.6% and falling, five prints remain, and the Aug 12 release is the first test. A one-tenth barrier correction on a 20.5¢ leg is worth more than most of the modelling this page does.

9 · THE TWO ROWS THAT ARE NOT MARKETS Mortgage 8 × 37.65 / 89 × 96.42 — an 81-point canyon with $4.57 of bids · stagflation, $19.16 of two-sided liquidity inside a point

Noted Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

Two rows this tab quotes with a straight face and shouldn’t. The mortgage 7.00% leg: five bid levels totalling 117.6 shares and $4.57, three ask levels totalling 127.0 shares, a reported midpoint of 48.5 that has never been transactable at any size. The stagflation leg: 225.1 shares bid ($12.72) and 105.8 offered ($6.44) inside a point of its 5.85 mid. Between them, $23.73 of executable liquidity supporting two rows that have appeared in this tab’s reasoning for ten days — the mortgage row as a transmission-channel anchor, the stagflation row as corroboration against the gold book. Both are hereby demoted to context. The mortgage barrier is genuinely live (PMMS 6.66% on Jul 30, a 6.70–7.25% implied band today), which is why ticket #9 rests a bid in the hole rather than deleting the row.

10 · THE SPX SKEW IS TEXTBOOK AND ITS FRONT RUNG IS A 0.93-SHARE QUOTE 22.1 × 54 / 23 × 0.93, then a gap to 28.8 — the whole offer side buys 84.0 shares for $23.90

Noted Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

The down-touch ladder does two contradictory things at once. Its implied volatilities are the most professional-looking numbers on the page — 24.6% / 27.9% / 33.3% across −17% / −23% / −31%, a clean monotone put skew — and its most-quoted rung is a fiction. The 6,200 leg reads 22.1 × 54 / 23 × 0.93, and above that best offer of 0.93 shares the stack jumps to 24 × 5 and then 28.8 × 78.1: buying the entire visible offer side gets you 84.0 shares for $23.90. Meanwhile the 5,200 leg carries 12,219.3 shares of bids at 7¢. Same event family, same expiry, same resolution source — a 476× depth difference between the near strike and the far one, with the size sitting on the tail. That inversion of the usual liquidity pattern is why ticket #5 sells the far rung and why the 6,200 mid is used only as a trigger, never as a price.

11 · THE BASKET THAT LOOKED FREE AND WASN'T ✅ HOUSE RULE #4 EARNED ITS KEEP Median-home-value legs sum to 95.75¢ on gamma and 101.20¢ on the CLOB — the arb was an artifact of the wrong endpoint

Noted Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

The seven buckets of the Sep 30 median-home-value market are mutually exclusive and exhaustive, so the YES legs must sum to $1. Gamma's prices sum to 95.75¢ — a 4.25-point free lunch, and exactly the kind of thing that gets published by a page that quotes discovery data as though it were executable. Walking all seven CLOB books instead: the best asks sum to 101.20¢, so buying the complete basket costs 1.2 points over par, and the best bids sum to 90.30¢, so selling it gives up 9.7. There is no arb; there is a 10.9-point round-trip spread, which is what a $10.7k-liquidity event with three dead tails actually looks like.

THE TICKET: No trade — this card exists as evidence. House rule #4 (gamma is discovery only, never a quoted price) has now caught a false arbitrage as well as the mortgage row's phantom 48.5 midpoint. Any future basket claim on this page must show the summed book sides, not the summed mids.

Ten dislocations, and the section’s scope changed tonight: it now covers the whole rates complex rather than the 10-year ladder, because the ladder is 8.4% of the complex by tradeable depth and was 100% of the tab’s attention. New tonight: the inverted gold smile (row 1 — the only ladder of three whose implied vol falls with strike, and the account owns its rich end), the depth ratio behind the kill-switch (row 2 — 857×), the hike complex’s coherence bound (row 3 — the books force P(both meetings) ≥ 17.5, and no arb survives it), the emergency-cut decomposition (row 4 — 42.0% of all cut probability is unscheduled, implying 80.0% of recession-worlds carry an intermeeting cut), the three-tails-one-price convergence (row 5), and the CPI barrier correction (row 8 — “greater than” plus one-decimal reporting moves the strike to 4.6%). Carried forward and re-verified: the mid we made ourselves (row 6), and the $3,582.74 / $268.35 split, now stated with its corollary — the mark is a defensible expectation, it is simply not liquidity (row 7). Demoted to context: the mortgage and stagflation rows, $23.73 of executable liquidity between them (row 9). Every quote walked at 22:29 UTC Aug 10, Monday post-close; implied volatilities inverted in-run from the driftless touch formula over the 143 days to Dec 31, using the barrier distances this tab already publishes. Eleven dislocations tracked (target 8–12) — #11 added tonight when a new complex, the Sep 30 median-US-home-value ladder, produced a 4.25-point basket "arb" on gamma prices that vanished the moment its seven CLOB books were actually walked.

Mids, touch, Can buy/Can sell and edge columns re-baked Aug 22, 2026 23:08 UTC (Saturday evening walk, bond market shut) — rung 1 ticked UP to 23.5 on a rebuilt 23 × 268 bid; rung 0 deepened to 64 × 630 / 70 × 30; SPX-6200 slid 16.15 → 11.6, the complex’s one big move; gold-6k re-rated 11.5 → 13.0; recession-2026 eased to 7.5; mort-7% 34.5 → 32.0; CPI-4.5 firmed to 14.0; no-cuts 86.15, Sep-hike 30.5, Dec-hike 28.0 unchanged; row notes still quote the Aug 10–11 deep walks, with a dated bracket where a later walk moved the book · model fairs are the Aug 10 exhibit — re-checked, not re-derived; the curve has moved +4 bp since (barrier now 77 bp) · verdict badges are the frozen Aug 10 calls (the withdrawn CHEAP on rungs 1–2, the smile artefact on the far rungs); only the mid, edge and depth columns are live
CHEAP the market price is below the model fair — the contract is undervalued, and these are the bets to make. Edge is negative because edge = mid − fair.RICH the market price is above fair — overvalued. If you hold it, that is a trim signal; if you do not, this page will not short it just because the model says so.FAIR inside ±2.5 points of fair — nothing to do.NO MODEL no diffusion behind the row; quoted for context and depth only.Value rank answers “which of these can I actually act on, and for how much?” — BUY ranks the CHEAP rows by capturable edge (|edge| × executable shares); TRIM ranks the RICH rows the account already owns. Everything else is unranked, with the reason in the cell.

Any Fed hike in 2026↗
Why NO MODEL, and why that is the finding: the union of the Sep and Dec legs must lie between the larger single leg (43.5, if a December hike only ever follows a September one) and their sum (76.0, if the two are mutually exclusive). The market prints 58.5 — inside the band. The hike complex is internally coherent, so there is no arbitrage here; there is only depth ($11k buy / $133k sell), which is more than the entire 10-year ladder offers. Sensitivity, and what actually moves these fairs. The mixture weight is the biggest lever and it is a judgement, not a measurement: at 100% martingale the 5.0% rung is 49.1 and at 100% AR(1) it is 20.9, so the whole CHEAP/RICH verdict on the two live rungs turns on a weight this page set to 50/50 because neither model survives on its own (methodology, “The two models”). Second lever: σ. Daily-change volatility in this series has essentially no autocorrelation (0.05), which means no trailing window forecasts forward vol — choosing 4.0 over 3.76 over 5.5 is a preference, and the 20-day estimate’s own 95% interval (2.90–5.51 bp) spans P(6%) = 2.0% to 11.9% at zero drift. Third: the barrier is monitored on the daily close, and the BGK shift that encodes this costs the ladder about 2 points at the near rungs — dropping it would make everything look cheaper. What this table no longer claims: that the Fed, SPX, gold, stagflation, CPI, recession and mortgage rows carry scenario information. They are marked NO MODEL. Their mids are live and their books are walked, and that is the entire contribution they make. Where the account sits: long 13,739 shares across four ladder rungs, marked $3,582.74, realisable $268.35 on tonight’s bid stacks (New #1). Books walked 22:29 UTC Aug 10, Monday post-close; every asset_id and timestamp verified in-run.

What a 6% Long Bond Does — The Transmission Map

Channel-by-channel model of the economy and the market at 30Y ≥ 6.00% · figures derived from sourced inputs listed in Methodology
ChannelMechanismWhere it lands
Mortgages & housingPMMS 30Y mortgage prices off the long end at a 145–200 bp spread. 6.00% Treasury ⇒ 7.4–8.0% mortgage. On a $336k loan (median-price, 20% down) the P&I payment goes $2,159 → ≈$2,440: +13%, on top of the cumulative repricing since 2021.Affordability through the 2023 lows toward early-1980s territory; existing-home lock-in deepens (nobody trades a 3% mortgage for 8%); transactions and starts stall; homebuilders and the real-estate services complex (the fund is short Z, EXPI, CBRE, CWK) take the direct hit.
The federal interest bill≈$41T of debt (the Polymarket peak-debt book priced $41T at 54¢ in the Jul 31 snapshot) rolling into a 6% long end while the deficit runs ≥$2T. Each +100 bp across the curve compounds toward +$300–400B/yr of interest expense as the stock rolls over.Interest expense — already past defense spending — becomes the deficit's fastest-growing line: more issuance ⇒ higher yields ⇒ more interest ⇒ more issuance. This loop is why a 6% touch is plausible without an inflation spiral: it's a term-premium revolt, the bond-vigilante mechanism Yardeni says already had its first Warsh test on Jul 30 (his note ran Jul 29).
Corporate credit & AI capex$1T+ of 2026 corporate issuance — much of it funding the AI datacenter buildout — reprices: IG lands ~7–7.5%, HY 9–11% once spreads widen (2022: IG +120 bp, HY +330 bp). Every hyperscaler lease and GPU depreciation schedule was underwritten at a 4-handle risk-free.The AI capex machine slows at the margin; long-duration equity gets the double hit (discount rate + capex doubt). The "AI bubble burst" book (19.7¢, Jul 31) and the semis-heavy model books on this site are the read-through. Refi wall 2027 turns from footnote to headline.
Banks & levered holdersA 30Y bought at 5.2% loses ≈11% at 6.0% (modified duration ≈15; convexity trims the realized loss) — that's the worst case, on the newest paper; seasoned 20-year holdings lose ≈9%. System AFS/HTM unrealized losses blow back through the 2023 peak (~$690B). The ~$1T hedge-fund Treasury basis trade is 2026's LDI: a VaR shock forces selling into a falling market.This is the disorderly path: SVB-2023 and UK-gilts-2022 both started here. Note what history actually says about the response: facilities and asset purchases, not emergency cuts — the BoE bought gilts with Bank Rate untouched; SVB got the BTFP. Watch the bank-failure books and repo/SRF usage — the accident, if it comes, shows up there first.
The stock marketSPX ≈7,490 at ~24–25x forward = 4.1% earnings yield vs a 5.4–5.6% 10-year in the scenario — an equity risk premium of ≈ −1.4%, unprecedented outside 1999–2000. Partial normalization (ERP to −0.4%…+0.2%) ⇒ 17.5–20x ⇒ SPX 5,300–6,100, a −19% to −29% drawdown; full normalization to +1% ERP ⇒ ~15x ⇒ under 4,800. History: 2022's hiking cycle took SPX −19% from a 21x start; Oct 2023's 5% 10-year took −10% from 19x; today's ~25x start is the amplifier. 1994's +250 bp left SPX flat — but from 15x.Long-duration megacap AI leads the downside (it led the upside for the same reason); relative winners: insurers reinvesting at 6%, short-duration value, energy. The SPX 6,200/5,800 touch legs sit at scenario-fair only under this page's calibration — their prices bundle every crash cause at once and carry little clean rates signal on their own.
The Fed's bindWarsh inherits 3.5% CPI (energy +15.7% y/y, war premium), a hiking bias (three dissents), and a long end that sells off anyway. Orderly path: he hikes (Sep 57.5¢), credibility slowly caps the long end — 1995, not 1994. Fiscal-dominance path: hikes don't help because the selloff is about supply, not expected inflation — late 2025 already showed cuts with a rising 30Y.The perverse trades: rate hikes that the long end ignores, then an accident answered with facilities (the UK 2022 template: gilts 3.6→5.1% in 3 sessions, up to £65B of purchases announced — ~£19B used — Bank Rate untouched, a government gone in 49 days). The Sep-hike book and the emergency-cut book can both matter in the same year — but the cut book is the rich way to own that story; the facilities era is exactly why.
The other sideBase case (model ≈82%): the pattern of 2026 holds — both spikes died within 5 bp of 5.20, June retraced 40 bp to 4.82%, core CPI is 2.6% and falling, each hike the market prices caps the long end a little more, and 5%+ all-in yields keep pulling insurers and pensions off the sidelines.If that's the world: the "how low" ladder (10Y below 3.9% at 8.8¢, Jul 31) and the SPX up-touches are the trades, and this page's basket burns to zero by New Year's. The BofA survey (62% expect a 6-handle within 12 months) cuts both ways — it's supportive, and it means the thesis is already consensus among the people who set the deep books' prices.

Resolution — Who Decides These Markets, and Where It Can Go Wrong

Ideas as of Aug 10, 2026 · re-checked Aug 22, 4:08 PM PT (Saturday evening walk) Four different definitions of “touch” across one tab — a daily par yield, an upper bound, a 1-minute candle, a futures settlement and a weekly survey · two of tonight’s tickets exist because of the resolution text, not despite it · read before sizing anything

THE LADDER — A DAILY PRINT, AND A WINDOW THAT OPENED IN 2025 The rule verbatim, and the 152 sessions that have already run against it

Idea added Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

“This market will resolve to ‘Yes’ if the Treasury 10-year yield reaches or is higher than the listed value for any date between [Nov 11, 2025 and Dec 31, 2026]. … The resolution source for this market is the Department of the treasury, specially the data listed under ‘Daily Treasury Par Yield Curve Rates’ for the column ‘10 Yr’.” Three consequences the tab has never spelled out. First, the barrier is a published daily par yield, not an intraday tick — a session that trades through 5.00% and prints 4.97% pays nothing, which is precisely the discrete-monitoring shift the model already charges about 2 points for. Second, the window is longer than the calendar year: it has been running since Nov 11, 2025. Third, the record so far is checkable and now checked — of the 152 sessions of 2026 in that file, the highest 10 Yr print is 4.75% on Jul 31, today’s 4.72% is the second-highest, and zero sessions have reached 4.80%. Rungs 0 through 5 are all still live; none of them has been quietly resolved by a print the tab missed while its yield pipeline was dark.

THE FED BOOKS — AN UPPER BOUND, A ROUNDING RULE, AND “CREDIBLE REPORTING” AGAIN 12.5 bp rounds UP to 25 · emergency cuts count toward the cut total · the year book cannot resolve NO until after Dec 8–9

Idea added Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

Every Fed row on this tab resolves on “the upper bound of the target federal funds range”, never the effective rate and never a Fed-speak signal. The meeting books resolve to the change “versus the level it was prior to” that meeting, and carry a rounding rule worth knowing: “if the target federal funds rate is changed to a level not expressed in the displayed options, the change will be rounded up to the nearest 25” — an unlikely 12.5 bp move resolves the 25-bp bracket, not the no-change one. The year book resolves YES if the upper bound rises “at any point between January 1, 2026 and the Fed’s [final] 2026 meeting”, primary source federalreserve.gov, “however a consensus of credible reporting may also be used” — the same soft clause that governs the largest-company books, here attached to something far less ambiguous. And the cut-count book explicitly counts emergency cuts outside scheduled meetings toward the total and stays open to Dec 31, 11:59 PM ET to catch them, which is exactly why the no-cuts leg and the emergency leg can be read as one distribution in ticket #3.

THREE MARKETS, THREE INCOMPATIBLE DEFINITIONS OF “TOUCH” SPX = a 1-minute candle high on Yahoo · gold = a CME Active Month settlement · mortgage = a weekly Freddie Mac survey

Idea added Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

The tab’s three touch ladders are quoted in the same column and monitored on three different clocks, which is the hidden reason their implied volatilities are not comparable. SPX resolves off “any 1-minute candle” high or low “as reflected in Yahoo Finance’s 1-minute interval data”, regular trading hours only — effectively continuous monitoring, and an instantaneous wick counts. Gold resolves off “the official CME settlement price for the Active Month” contract, where the Active Month is the nearest delivery-cycle month (Feb/Apr/Jun/Aug/Oct/Dec) that is not the spot month, and it rolls automatically on the First Position Date — so an intraday spike to $5,000 that settles at $4,960 pays nothing, and the contract being measured changes underneath the holder several times before Dec 31. The mortgage row resolves off the weekly Freddie Mac PMMS — roughly 20 observations left in the year, the coarsest monitoring on the page, which makes a barrier the spread band already straddles genuinely uncertain. Same word, three instruments, and only one of them is near-continuous.

WHERE THE TAIL TICKETS CAN GO WRONG — REVISIONS, ADVANCE ESTIMATES, AND A SETTLEMENT THAT LANDS IN 2027 The recession leg may not resolve until the Q4 advance estimate, weeks after every other row on this tab has paid

Idea added Aug 10, 2026 · Reviewed Aug 22, 2026View on Polymarket ↗

Four ways the short-tail basket in ticket #10 can be right about the world and still lose. One: the recession leg resolves on advance estimates and reaches back to Q2 2025 — a revision that turns an old quarter negative can trigger it, and it “will stay open until the Advance estimate of Q4 2026 is published”, which lands well after Dec 31. Capital is committed past the horizon the rest of the tab assumes. Two: the inflation leg freezes on the year-end release — “any revisions to previously released CPI figures will not be counted” after it — so a hot print that is later revised away still resolves YES, and the ticket is short exactly that asymmetry. Three: the year-end state-of-the-economy book resolves on the year-end U-3 and CPI-U prints and defaults if they are not published by Jan 31, 2027, so a data-release disruption is a live settlement risk on a $19.16 book. Four: the ladder’s own source is a government web page that this site has already watched go stale for eleven days. Every one of these is a reason the tail carries are capped at $750 combined rather than sized to their apparent edge — and prediction markets can resolve against you on oracle judgment alone.

Four cards, all rewritten tonight against the live market descriptions rather than from memory, and two of them changed a ticket. The ladder card produced the verified fact that zero of 152 sessions in the 2026 par-yield file have printed a 10 Yr at or above 4.80% — the year’s high is 4.75% on Jul 31, today is 4.72% — which is what makes the rung-0 watch (#11) a liquidity problem rather than a modelling one. The inflation card produced the “greater than” plus one-decimal reading that lifts the effective barrier to 4.6%, which is the reason ticket #6 exists at all. Unchanged and worth repeating: these are not exchange-cleared instruments; resolution runs through published data and, on the Fed rows, through a “consensus of credible reporting” clause that has already misfired elsewhere on this site. The 10-year rungs monitor on daily closes, gold on CME settlements of a contract that rolls, SPX on 1-minute candles, mortgages on a weekly survey — four monitoring frequencies, one column of prices, and a comparison between them is only ever approximate. Fictitious research page; nothing here is financial advice.

Methodology, Sources & Honesty Box

The touch model

P(30Y prints ≥6.00% on a daily close by Dec 31) uses the exact drifted first-passage probability — Φ((μT−a)/σ√T) + e^(2μa/σ²)·Φ((−a−μT)/σ√T) — with a Broadie–Glasserman–Kou barrier shift (+0.5826·σ√Δt) because the ladder resolves on one daily print, not the intraday path. That distinction is not pedantry: in Oct 2023 the 10-year famously “hit 5%” intraday (5.02%) while the daily par yield peaked at 4.98% — the most celebrated touch of the last cycle would have resolved NO on these markets’ terms. Re-derived Aug 10 on a live curve for the first time since Jul 30: a = 75 bp from the 5.25% close, T = 101 trading days, σ = 4.0 bp/day (measured: 20d 4.20, 40d 3.80, 60d 3.89, full-2026 3.76). With zero drift, P = 5.4%. With 2026’s realised +0.258 bp/day carried forward, P = 16.2% — but 67% of that number is the drift, and the drift has a standard error of 0.306, so t = 0.84, p = 0.40; its 95% interval alone maps to P = 0.8%–68.2%. This tab now leads with the martingale number and shows the trend case beside it. Two prior errors, disclosed: the old central case of 18% never reproduced from its own stated inputs (a = 79, T = 105, σ = 5.5, +15 bp gives 20.8%), and the σ = 5.5 it used was attributed to a July vol spike that the data does not contain — July’s 30Y daily-change sd was 3.58 bp, the fourth-lowest month of 2026.

Conditionals — and why they are disclosed

The per-row P|6% / P|no-6% decomposition is retired. Its conditionals were hand-set, its P6 was mostly drift, and the “cal.” rows had P|no-6% calibrated so that fair ≡ mid — carrying, by construction, no information at all. The ladder is now priced by two models fitted to the 2026 10-year directly, and every non-ladder row is marked NO MODEL rather than given a fair this page cannot defend. One published conditional is explicitly withdrawn (see New #11): the claim that long-end selloffs widen 30s10s, which justified rung 3’s 30% conditional, fails on 2026 — the ten largest 30Y selloff days widened the spread five times, mean +0.7 bp, and β(Δ10Y on Δ30Y) = 1.056 implies bear-flattening.

Rows marked “cal.”

Gone, and so is the label. Where a row has no yield model behind it — the Fed complex, the SPX down-touch ladder, gold, stagflation, CPI, recession, the mortgage row — the table now prints NO MODEL and leaves the model columns blank. That is a demotion of about half the table, and it is honest: those books move on things this page does not model, and calibrating a fair to their own mid was a way of appearing to price them without doing so.

The two models, and the mixture

Martingale: zero-drift random walk on the 10-year, σ = 4.4 bp/day (measured 4.26–4.60 across windows), same first-passage machinery and BGK shift. AR(1): fitted on 2026, φ = 0.98089, attractor 4.549%, half-life 35.9 trading days. The fair is their equal-weight mixture, and the honest reason is that neither survives on its own. The AR(1) is probably Dickey–Fuller small-sample bias with a story attached: simulating true random walks at n = 151 produces a φ̂ at or below 0.98089 52.8% of the time, and an ADF test has only ~10% power against it here — so “the series mean-reverts” is not something this data can establish. The martingale, meanwhile, ignores that yields have looked mean-reverting to everyone pricing these books all year. The mixture lands within a point of the market on both live rungs (35.0 vs 34.00, 13.8 vs 14.15), which is what an efficient market facing genuine model uncertainty should look like. We are not claiming the mixture is right. We are claiming the market is not obviously wrong, which is what this tab spent ten days asserting.

The smile, and what a constant σ does to it

Inverting each rung to the σ that makes it fair at zero drift gives 1.94 / 3.11 / 3.38 / 4.48 / 4.97 / 5.87 bp per day across the 4.8 → 6.00 strikes — a clean monotone smile. Against a rising smile, any constant-σ model must call the near rungs cheap and the far rungs rich. This tab reported exactly that pattern for ten days and read it as a finding; it was the assumption looking at itself. The implied-σ column is published so the smile is visible rather than laundered through a fair.

Books, and how thin they are

Every executable quote was CLOB-verified (bid/ask/depth, asset_id matched, timestamp checked) at 22:29 UTC Aug 10; gamma-only prices are never shown as executable, which is why the mortgage row wears NO BOOK. The depth numbers matter more than the prices. The 10-year ladder marks $3,582.74 and liquidates into its own bid stacks for $268.35: the 5.0% leg fills 1,047 of 8,880 shares at a 17.1¢ VWAP and 88% finds no bid at any price, while the 5.2% and 5.5% legs do fill in full — into 25,015 and 16,549 shares of penny bids. A further caution this tab did not previously make: the account’s own orders move these books. Tonight’s 698-share lift at 25.21¢ cleared the offer stack 35 minutes before the snapshot and left an 18-point air pocket above the quote, so the 34.0 mid is partly our own footprint; the tape’s last trade is 27.0.

Scoring the account against this tab

Seven Treasury legs were bought off these cards, and tonight’s revision makes most of them look luckier rather than smarter. Marked against the fairs published at the time: rung 1 (5.0%) filled at a 21.56¢ blended basis against a 41.7% fair then, and a 35.0% mixture now — 20 points inside the old fair, 13 inside the new one, marking +$1,015.52 and realisable for $178.67. Rung 2 (5.2%) at 7.53¢ against 17.5% then, 13.8% now, +$189.57 marked. Rung 3 (5.5%) at 5.51¢ against 5.8% then, 3.4% now — at model then, above it now, +$32.31. Rung 0 (4.8%) at a 70.5¢ basis against 65.4% then, 77.6% now — the only leg the revision helps, −$11.63. The two low-ladder NO legs (3.6% and 3.7%) are short the same tail the ladder is long. This is one correlated $3.6k bet, not seven independent ones, and the honest summary of the ten days is that the page called a direction correctly and its stated reason for the call does not survive its own re-derivation.

What would falsify this tab

The September FOMC passing without a hike while the 30Y closes back under 5.00% kills the premise outright. Beyond that, tonight’s specific commitments: (1) if the 5.0% rung’s bid side does not carry $1,000 within two weeks, the “+$1,015 gain” this page printed tonight was never a gain and the tab says so permanently; (2) if the implied-σ smile flattens — near and far strikes converging on one σ — then the constant-σ model this tab retired was defensible after all and the CHEAP calls should be reinstated; (3) if the 10-year grinds to 4.85–4.90% and rung 1 does not reprice above 45¢, the martingale leg of the mixture is what is wrong. Snapshot built Aug 10, 2026 22:29 UTC — Monday post-close, the session this tab argued against its own book. Fictitious research page; nothing here is investment advice.

Bitcoin — The Two Ladders

Bitcoin printed $77,033 on Coinbase at 23:08 UTC Aug 22, 2026 — off 1.7% from Friday’s $78,326 close and 3.1% under the $79,500 August high set Friday afternoon, but still up 22.6% in six sessions from the Aug 16 close of $62,837. The catalysts are dated and sourced: the Aug 19 White House meeting (Trump with Coinbase and Ripple executives plus the SEC and CFTC chairs, directing Congress to “take the next step” on the stalled CLARITY Act), the SEC’s proposed crypto rule changes of Aug 18, the CFTC’s Aug 20 statement that it will build a crypto-asset regime under existing authorities, and Bessent’s Aug 19 pledge to “at least double” Treasury buybacks of longer-dated paper. This page prices two Polymarket touch ladders: “What price will Bitcoin hit in August” (21 legs, window closes Aug 31 23:59 ET, 9.2 days out) and “What price will Bitcoin hit before 2027” (30 live legs, Dec 31 23:59 ET, 131.2 days out). Both resolve on a Binance BTC/USDT 1-minute candle high or low — not Coinbase, which runs a few tens of dollars under. The model is a driftless lognormal touch probability, P = 2·N(−|ln(B/S)|/(σ√T)), at the full-year realised σ = 2.35%/day, with σ = 2.0 and 3.0 shown beside it. The skew steepened as spot slid: every Dec up-rung from $80k to $120k trades at an implied σ of 1.70–2.28%/day, the Dec down-rungs from $55k to $25k at 2.69–4.37% — the market still pays for the pull-back and discounts the continuation, and the December up-mids fell FASTER than the model fairs did, flipping the $110k back to CHEAP and pushing $90k to −13.8. The account sat out a zero-trade window holding TEN BTC legs marked $4,823.45 — 33.5% of the $14,408.79 positions value — that walk out for $4,475.60, a 7.2% haircut. The five August sweep legs bled on to −$1,029.24. No new trades are published here without a walked bid and ask in dollars. Fictitious research page — not financial advice.

Every Market — Fair Value, and Whether There Is a Market At All

Mids, Book × size and depth columns baked from the CLOB at 23:08 UTC Aug 22, 2026 (asset_id = each market’s YES token) — 51 legs: 21 August (T = 9.20 d), 30 December (T = 131.2 d), reach legs ascending then dip legs descending within each ladder · Model fair is the driftless reflection-principle touch probability at σ = 2.35%/day (349-day realised), spot $77,033.39 Coinbase; row notes carry the σ 2.0 / 3.0 brackets and the implied σ · mids refresh every 60 seconds and edge / verdict recompute off the baked fair; fairs do not decay with the clock (a stale T over-states fair, flattering live CHEAP badges) · rows with mid < 2.0 and fair < 0.5 wear TAIL and publish no edge — a 1–2¢ quote is a funding floor, not a probability · Can buy / Can sell are the full ask and bid sides in dollars · Value rank = Σ(fair − ask) × size over every ask level priced under fair, from the raw book
CHEAP the market price is below the model fair — the contract is undervalued, and these are the bets to make. Edge is negative because edge = mid − fair.RICH the market price is above fair — overvalued. If you hold it, that is a trim signal; if you do not, this page will not short it just because the model says so.FAIR inside ±2.5 points of fair — nothing to do.TAIL mid under 2¢ and fair under 0.5 — no model resolution; quoted for context and depth only.Value rank answers “which of these can I actually act on, and for how much?” — BUY ranks the CHEAP rows by capturable edge (Σ(fair − ask) × size over the ask levels under fair); TRIM ranks the RICH rows the account already owns. Everything else is unranked, with the reason in the cell.

Sensitivity. The σ is the whole model. At σ 2.0 / 2.35 / 3.0 the Dec $100k leg is 29.4 / 37.1 / 48.4, the Dec $90k leg 55.4 / 61.4 / 69.3, the Dec $60k dip 24.2 / 31.9 / 43.5, the Aug $85k reach 21.7 / 29.3 / 41.0, the Aug $75k dip 47.2 / 54.0 / 63.1. At σ 2.0 (the 60-day realised) the $80k (−2.1) and $110k (+2.2) legs go FAIR and the $100k leg sits at −2.9, still CHEAP by four-tenths of a point, while $85k (−7.6), $90k (−5.9) and $95k (−4.8) stay CHEAP; at σ 3.0 (the 10-day realised rounded up) every RICH down-rung goes FAIR or CHEAP except $35k (+4.6) and $30k (+3.8), which stay RICH. The windows: 10d 2.97%, 20d 2.39%, 30d 2.26%, 60d 2.00%, 90d 2.12%, 349d 2.35%; Parkinson 20d 2.12%, 60d 2.04%. The house picks the longest window because the December horizon is 132 days and the shortest window is one week of catalysts — but that is a choice, and the table’s verdicts are its consequence. What the model does not price: drift (a +25% week is not a zero-mean draw), regime change (the Aug 18–20 rule-making is a level shift in the distribution, not a sample from it), and the Binance/Coinbase basis (tens of dollars, which matters only for the Aug $80k leg). Capturable edge walks the raw ask side up to the σ 2.35 fair and sums (fair − ask) × size level by level; at σ 2.0 the $90k figure is $360, the $100k figure $25, and the Aug $75k figure zero (fair 47.2, under the 51 offer).

Methodology, Sources & Honesty Box

The touch model

Driftless lognormal, reflection principle: P(touch B before T) = 2·N(−|ln(B/S)| / (σ√T)), S = $78,536.24 (Coinbase BTC-USD, 22:18 UTC Aug 21), σ in %/day, T in days. Symmetric by construction — the same formula prices a reach and a dip — and driftless by choice, because no drift estimate on Bitcoin has a t-statistic worth publishing. The model is the same first-passage machinery the Treasuries tab uses without the barrier shift: these markets resolve on 1-minute candles, so the continuous-monitoring assumption is close to exact.

T, and what the clock does

T_aug = 10.24 days, to Aug 31 23:59 ET = Sep 1 04:00Z; T_dec = 132.3 days, to Dec 31 23:59 ET = Jan 1 05:00Z; both measured from the 22:18Z snapshot. Fairs are baked at those T and do not decay on the page. As the clock runs the true touch probability falls while the baked fair stays put, so the baked fair is too high: a live mid that tracks the shrinking T looks ever cheaper against it. Stale T flatters every live CHEAP badge and under-reports every RICH one — the August rows worst, since ten days is a third of their window by Aug 25. Re-bake before trusting any August verdict after Aug 25.

σ, and its sensitivity

House σ = 2.35%/day, the 349-day close-to-close realised (45% annualised). Alternatives measured: 10d 2.97%, 20d 2.39%, 30d 2.26%, 60d 2.00%, 90d 2.12%; Parkinson 20d 2.12%, 60d 2.04%. The σ 2.0 and σ 3.0 columns in the data bracket every fair in the table; the row notes print the implied σ — the daily vol at which the model reproduces the mid — so the reader can see which σ the market is using rather than argue about which one the page should. Every verdict is a statement about 2.35 vs that number.

Binance vs Coinbase

All 51 markets resolve on the Binance BTC/USDT 1-minute candle high (reach) or low (dip). Spot, candles and realised vol on this page are Coinbase BTC-USD. Binance USDT prints typically run a few tens of dollars above Coinbase USD; the page discloses and does not adjust. It matters for exactly one row tonight — Aug $80k reach, where Coinbase’s 79,500 high is $500 under the rung — and it is why that card says check the market page before the book.

The 1–2¢ tail floor

Rows with mid < 2.0¢ and fair < 0.5 are labelled TAIL and publish no edge. The implied σ of 3–9%/day on those rows (Aug $40k dip 6.06%, Dec $5k dip 9.53%, Dec $1M reach 8.27%) is the price of warehousing an unsellable contract, not a vol forecast. Counting those as RICH would add seventeen badges (ten August, seven December) to the table that no one can act on. The Dec $25k dip (2.5¢, +2.5 exactly) and the Dec $170k–$200k reach legs (2.05–2.35¢) sit just above the threshold and wear FAIR; they are floor pricing too.

Books, and capturable edge

Every quote is a CLOB /book snapshot with asset_id matched to the market’s YES token, August at 22:17–22:18Z and December at 22:08–22:09Z. Can buy = the full ask side in dollars, Can sell = the full bid side; Market? is DEEP / OK / THIN / NO MARKET on those. Capturable edge for a CHEAP row = Σ(fair − askᵢ) × sizeᵢ over every ask level priced under the σ 2.35 fair, from the raw book — what a buyer walking the offer up to fair would pocket at fair: $90k $2,250 (33,150 sh), $100k $1,234 (25,747), $85k $1,162 (15,066), $95k $508 (10,231), $80k $224 (12,771), $110k $183 (12,121), Aug $75k dip $18 (2,721), Aug $72.5k dip $0 (58). Walk-outs for held legs are from the same books: all nine fill in full.

What the model cannot do

It has no drift: a +25% five-session move is, to the model, a draw from a zero-mean distribution, and the next five are too. It has no regime: the SEC Aug 18 / White House Aug 19 / CFTC Aug 20 sequence is a level shift in the rule set, and the model treats Aug 21 like any other day at 2.35%. It has no skew of its own, which is precisely why it can see the market’s: up 1.5–2.3, down 2.5–4.4. The skew is a claim — the market’s claim that the rip mean-reverts — and this page reports it rather than trading against it. Sixteen CHEAP/RICH badges on the December ladder are one disagreement about σ, repeated.

Sources

Coinbase BTC-USD spot, 24h stats and daily candles (Aug 14–21) · Polymarket CLOB /book and /midpoint for all 51 legs; data-api /positions and /value for the account · Yahoo Finance Aug 21 (open $73,013, +15.2% w/w, −36.1% y/y, ATH $126,198.07 Oct 6, 2025) · Motley Fool Aug 21 (+6.34% to $77,205, 24h $72,500–$79,320, COIN +7.53%, XRP +11.87%) · White House meeting Aug 19, SEC proposal Aug 18, CFTC statement Aug 20, Treasury buyback announcement Aug 19 as reported. Live refresh adds 51 CLOB midpoint calls per 60 s to the page’s existing 36. Fictitious research page — not financial advice.

What would falsify this tab

A Dec up-rung mid rising to its σ 2.35 fair with spot unchanged — the market adopting the house σ — would make the skew disappear and the CHEAP badges with it; that is the test of the claim, and it is the market’s to pass. Beyond that, tonight’s specific commitments: (1) no BTC adds at any rung while the complex is over a third of the book; (2) re-walk all nine bid sides every run and print the haircut; (3) the Aug $80k resolution status checked before any August ticket; (4) re-bake the August fairs before Aug 25 or stop quoting them.

Decision Tracking — Scoring Every Executed Call

When a recommendation from this site gets actioned (add-on, new position, or sell), it moves here as a permanent ledger entry. Each row is re-scored live against the current market so the effectiveness of every decision is measurable — sells score the money saved by exiting, adds score P&L since the fill. The twice-daily run appends new executions with actual average fills from the trade history.

Reading the total. The TOTAL row sums every decision to the Aug 22 23:08 UTC Saturday evening-walk books: +$9,163.49 across the full 230-row column — down $356.77 from the morning walk, on ZERO new rows: the contraction is entirely re-marks, and nearly all of it is the December BTC give-back flowing through the anchor’s BUY rows as the $100k mid slid 26.0 → 22.5 (the Aug 20 anchor adds at 16¢, rows 214/216, still grade green but ≈$300 less so). Against that, the OpenAI rebuy row improved $38 on the bounce to 18.0, and the spike-sell holds +$275.50, still the best print in the ledger (row 205). 202 rows carry live re-marks; the remainder are frozen at resolution or have no quotable book and keep their last honest print. ZERO rows were added this window — the first fully empty tape since Aug 15.

How scoring works — SELLS: Since Decision = (exit price − current price) × shares. Positive means the exit saved money (the position kept falling); negative means it left money on the table so far. Final verdicts land at resolution. ADDS/BUYS: Since Decision = (current − fill) × shares, plus realized payout at resolution. Add-side executions are detected and appended automatically by the twice-daily run from the account's trade history — with actual average fills replacing estimates.

Scoring: for a retired BUY idea, if the price fell after we dropped it, the drop was right (GOOD DROP); if it kept climbing, we dropped it too early (TOO EARLY) — and the size of the move is the tuition. Retired SELL ideas score inversely. This ledger plus the executed ledger above covers every decision path: acted on, or walked away from.
